# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-04-07_TotalExtraction_30412536.pdf
 
2101 O’Neil Ave., Room 202 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
(307) 433-9730   
downtowncheyenne.com 
 
 
Background: 
Attached is the amended budget for ﬁscal year 2026 (July 1 to June 30) for the Cheyenne DDA. The proposed 
Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral: April 13, 2026 
• 
Finance Committee: April 21, 2026 
• 
Final consideration: April 27, 2026 
The adopted budget for FY2026 was $728,450, established based on prior years' operating expenses. As DDA 
operations and key initiatives expanded throughout the year, additional resources were required beyond the 
original budget projections. 
Wyo. Stat. 15-9-218 requires a DDA to adopt a budget, with administrative review conducted in accordance 
with each municipality's policies. The DDA has historically managed budget adjustments through quarterly 
amendments. Going forward, the DDA will align its budgeting process to ensure full statutory compliance on 
an annual basis. 
The proposed changes to the FY2026 DDA Budget are summarized as follows:  
Revenue 
• 
$5,000.00 increase to (405) Laramie County to account for a clerical error at original adoption (grant of 
$10,000.00 was awarded for FY26). 
• 
$92,824 increase to (450) Revenue from Reserves to cover increase to FY26 expense items 
Expenses 
• 
$4,000.00 increase to (510) Accounting & Audit for 2026 1099 ﬁlings and FY26 contracted CPA services 
not previously budgeted 
• 
$37,250.00 increase to (710) Downtown Planter Program for FY26 (to June 2026) expanded vendor 
scope (additional ﬂowers and planters) 
• 
$5,624.00 increase to (711) Downtown Banner Initiative for design (depending on Board sourcing 
decision) and printing costs for summer seasonal banners 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Sophia Maes, DDA Executive Director 
Subject: 
FY2026 DDA Budget Amendment Resolution 
Date: 
April 13, 2026 
 
 

2101 O’Neil Ave., Room 202 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
(307) 433-9730
downtowncheyenne.com
•
$22,275.00 increase to (720) Sponsorship for FOTP sponsorship (previous accounting split pushed 2025
FOTP expenses into FY26). Moving full Sponsorship expense to FY26 for full payment.
•
$11,175.00 increase to (735) Façade Improvement Program (FIP) to cover anticipated FIP awards for
remainder of FY26
•
$17,500.00 increase to (735) Capital Improvement Grant (CIG) to cover anticipated CIG awards for
remainder of FY26
This amendment adds $97,824 to operating budget line items, funded through a combination of Reserves and 
a Laramie County grant, bringing the total FY2026 budget to $826,274. 
The attached amended budget spreadsheet reﬂects the full scope of adjustments, including additional line 
items requested by the DDA Board and incorporated following the March 19, 2026 staﬀ report. The DDA 
Board approved the amended budget at its March 19, 2026 Regular Board meeting. 
Attachments: 
1.
Resolution
2.
Exhibit A: DDA Approved Proposed Budget Amendment (4/2026)- Fiscal Year 2026
3.
FY2026 DDA Approved Budgets

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION APPROVING AN INCREASE OF THE BUDGET OF 
THE CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY FOR 
THE FISCAL YEAR BEGINNING JULY 1, 2025, AND ENDING JUNE 30, 
2026." 
 
WHEREAS, on July 9, 1984, the Governing Body approved Ordinance No. 2804 creating 
the Downtown Development Authority (hereafter referred to as “Authority”) and Downtown 
Development District (hereafter referred to as “District”); 
 
WHEREAS, the proposed budget for the Authority for the fiscal year beginning July 1, 
2025, and ending June 30, 2026, was approved by the Governing Body on May 27, 2025 under 
Resolution 6468; 
 
WHEREAS, Section 2.76.050(C) of the city code provides that the DDA budget shall be 
subject to alteration, modification and approval by the Governing Body in accordance with the 
policies of the City; 
 
WHEREAS, the Authority has identified an unanticipated increase of ninety-seven 
thousand eight hundred twenty-four dollars ($97,824.00) in operational costs for the current fiscal 
year, resulting from staff turnover and the expansion and addition of programs that were not 
reflected in prior assessments; 
 
WHEREAS, at its March 19, 2026, meeting the DDA Board approved the proposed budget 
increase of ninety-seven thousand eight hundred twenty-four dollars ($97,824.00) for the fiscal 
year beginning July 1, 2025, and ending June 30, 2026, a copy of which is attached to this 
Resolution as Exhibit A and incorporated herein; and 
 
WHEREAS, City staff has reviewed the proposed budget increase received from the 
DDA. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the proposed budget increase of the Downtown 
Development Authority for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2025, and ending June 30, 2026, 
as set forth in the attached Exhibit A, entitled “Downtown Development Authority Budget 
Amendment – Fiscal Year 2026” in the amount of  ninety-seven thousand eight hundred twenty-
four dollars ($97,824.00), increasing the total budget amount to eight hundred twenty-six 
thousand two hundred seventy-four dollars ($826,274.00), is hereby approved.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-04-07_TotalExtraction_78559177.pdf
PLANNING &
 
DEVELOPMENT
 
2101 O’Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
(307) 637-6282 
cheyennecity.org 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Seth Lloyd 
Subject: PUDC-26-59 / Stansberry Farms – Future Land Use Map Amendment, Overruling of Planning 
Commission Action 
Date: 
April 13, 2026 
______________________________________________________________________________________________________________ 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Resolution overruling a Planning Commission disapproval of a proposed   
Future Land Use Map Amendment (or Master Plan Amendment).  This is needed to fulfil Wyoming State 
Statute 15-1-506 and allow the Future Land Use Map Amendment to be considered by the Governing 
Body.    
 
The Planning Commission heard a future land use map amendment at their April 6, 2026, meeting and 
voted 3-2 in favor of the amendment. State Statute requires 4 ‘yes’ votes from the Planning 
Commission. Thus, the request was disapproved according to Wyoming State Statute (15-1-505). 
Wyoming State Statute allows the Governing Body to overrule the Planning Commission and consider 
plans or plan amendments the Planning Commission disapproves. 
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral   
April 13, 2026 
• 
Public Services Committee  
April 20, 2026 
• 
Final Consideration 
 
April 27, 2026 
 
 
Attachments: 
1. 
Resolution 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION OVERRULING THE PLANNING COMMISSION’S 
DISAPPROVAL OF AN AMENDMENT TO THE FUTURE LAND USE 
MAP OF PLANCHEYENNE CHANGING THE LAND USE DESIGNATION 
FROM MIXED-USE RESIDENTIAL TO MIXED-USE EMPLOYMENT 
FOR PROPERTY GENERALLY LOCATED SOUTH OF I-80, EAST OF 
SOUTHWEST DRIVE." 
 
 
WHEREAS, on April 6, 2026, the Planning Commission considered a Master Plan 
amendment for an amendment to the Land Use Plan for PlanCheyenne, PUDC-26-59 (changing 
the land use designation from Mixed-use Residential to Mixed-use Employment for property 
generally located south of I-80, east of Southwest Drive), where the final vote of 3-2 failed to reach 
the majority required by Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-505; and 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-506, the Governing Body has the authority to 
overrule the Planning Commission’s disapproval. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body hereby formally overrules the Planning 
Commission’s disapproval of a Land Use Plan for PlanCheyenne, PUDC-26-59, and authorizes 
the item to proceed for final consideration.  
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2026. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
KYLIE SODEN, CITY CLERK 
 
11:09 am, Apr 08 2026


--- END OF FILE ---

Subject: PUDC-26-59 / Stansberry Farms – Future Land Use Map Amendment  
Date: 
April 13, 2026 
______________________________________________________________________________________________________________ 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Future Land Use Map Amendment (or Comprehensive Plan 
Amendment).  This is approved by Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral   
April 13, 2026 
• 
Public Services Committee  
April 20, 2026 
• 
Final Consideration 
 
April 27, 2026 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their April 6, 2026, meeting and voted 3-2 in favor of the 
amendment. Due to disparities between Planning Commission bylaws and State Statute, it was 
determined that the 3-2 vote was final and that 4 ‘yes’ votes were required to approve the motion 
(therefore the motion failed). Approval of this motion was essential for the associated zone change, and 
the Planning Commission used this determination to heavily influence their recommendation on the 
associated zone change (see the zone change staff report for more information on that item). A 
Resolution overruling the Planning Commission’s disapproval is required and is a separate item on the 
Council agenda. 
 
State Statute and Planning Commission Bylaws: 
Wyoming State Statute 15-1-505 (in part):  
“The adoption of the plan or any part, amendment or addition shall be by resolution carried by 
the affirmative vote of not less than a majority of the commission.” (emphasis added) 
Planning Commission Bylaws (Chapter VI): 
“Section 2. Official Business. To transact official business, a quorum must be present. A majority 
of those present and voting may approve or disapprove official business. The Chair shall not vote 
as a member of the Planning Commission unless a tie vote occurs of the majority present and 
voting.” (emphasis added) 
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and unchanged. 
 
Public Comment 
Between the publication of the Planning Commission Staff Report and the publication of this memo 
(April 8, 2026), Staff has received no new inquires or comments from the public on this project. The 
project page on the website has received 80 views and no comments.

Resolution 
2. 
Planning Commission Staff Report 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AMENDING THE FUTURE LAND USE MAP OF 
PLANCHEYENNE CHANGING THE LAND USE DESIGNATION FROM 
MIXED-USE RESIDENTIAL TO MIXED-USE EMPLOYMENT FOR 
PROPERTY GENERALLY LOCATED SOUTH OF I-80, EAST OF 
SOUTHWEST DRIVE." 
 
 
WHEREAS, PlanCheyenne, as amended by PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress 
adopted by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne by Resolution No. 5573, is the Master 
Plan for the City of Cheyenne and the Comprehensive Plan for portions of Laramie County adopted 
by both the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County Board of County 
Commissioners; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the intent of the Future Land Use plan is not to predetermine specific land 
uses for individual parcels or specific locations for uses or facilities, but to provide a general 
framework for neighborhoods and activity centers organized around roadway networks and major 
parks and open space; 
 
 
WHEREAS, John Sayers, a resident within Cheyenne, has initiated an amendment to the 
Future Land Use map to amend the land use category from Mixed-use Residential to Mixed-use 
Commercial for land legally described as Tract 1, H and B Tracts, and Lot 2, Block 1, H and B 
Tracts, 2nd Filing, Cheyenne, Wyoming; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendments approved by the Governing Body pursuant to the criteria 
set forth on Page 18 of PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress, as amended; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Laramie County Board of County Commissioners reviewed the proposed 
future land use map amendment and were given the opportunity to make comments; 
 
 
WHEREAS, a public hearing to receive public input and comments on the proposed Future 
Land Use map amendment for the above-described property was held before the City of Cheyenne 
Planning Commission on April 6, 2026; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendment processes and procedures required by PlanCheyenne, as 
amended, have been followed with regard to the request by John Sayers to amend the Future Land 
Use map category for the above-described property. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne Governing Body hereby amends the 
Land Use Plan for PlanCheyenne changing the land use category from Mixed-use Residential to 
Mixed-use Employment, as shown in Exhibit A, in accordance with the procedures outlined in 
PlanCheyenne and Wyoming Statutes § 15-1-503 and § 15-1-505; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that staff is authorized to amend affected maps of 
PlanCheyenne to reflect the aforementioned changes; and 
9:15 am, Apr 08 2026

 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this resolution shall become effective after its 
passage, approval, and signing by the Mayor.

2026.

RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to deny the Resolution amending the Future Land Use Plan for a portion of land south of I-80 and 
east of Southwest Drive as shown in Exhibit A from Mixed-use Residential to Mixed-Use Employment, due 
the request not meeting review criteria 1, 2, 7, and 8 – as residential development is needed in the 
Cheyenne region and the entire area framed by Lincolnway, the BNSF Railroad, College, and I-25 is 
anticipated to develop with a mix of urban residential and commercial uses. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting amending the Future Land Use Map for roughly 94 acres of land from Mixed-use 
Residential to Mixed-use Employment to accommodate a zone change  to LI Light Industrial for the purpose of 
unspecified mixed-use employment uses.  
 
The property is shown as Mixed-use Residential in the Future Land Use Plan. The Mixed‐Use Residential 
category promotes self‐supporting neighborhoods that primarily contain housing. A range of residential 
housing types, such as single‐family residences, duplexes, patio homes, townhomes, apartments, 
condominiums, and live‐work units, should be built on a majority of any site within this category. The Mixed-
use Residential designation considers the MUR Mixed-use Residential zone as the optimal zone district; 
however, various residential zones and lighter-intensity commercial zones may also be appropriate. 
 
A concurrent application for a zoning map amendment has been submitted. The zoning map amendment will 
accompany the future land use amendment at City Council. If the application for the zoning map amendment 
is approved, the applicant will be required to submit a site plan application for the new development.

ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve an amendment to the Future Land Use Map of the 
Comprehensive Plan, the Commission must make the following considerations outlined in the 2014 
Community Plan (Build Chapter page 3-3).  A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) BENEFICIAL OR ADDRESSES ISSUES - The proposed amendment is beneficial to the 
community or addresses issues not foreseen during the planning process.    
 
This review criterion has two parts, each of which will be addressed individually. 
 
Regarding if the proposed amendment is beneficial to the community: 
The proposed amendment has been requested to allow LI Light Industrial zoning. The Land use 
category requested supports business‑campus style development focused primarily on office and 
light industrial uses and is similar to the development patterns of North Range, Swan Ranch and 
High Plains Business Parks. These areas are intended to incorporate open spaces, parks, and 
pedestrian connections. Retail and service uses may be included as supportive secondary uses to 
support the campus style development and its employees. Higher‑intensity employment uses are 
encouraged near major roadways and residential development is typically not supported in these 
districts. 
 
Regarding if the proposed amendment addresses unforeseen issues: 
The subject property (and surrounding area) is hemmed in by regional transportation routes and 
lacks water mains. However, these were exiting conditions at the time the comprehensive plan 
was developed, meaning that the original plan likely considered these issues and yet still came to 
the conclusion a unified residential neighborhood was appropriate for this area. Also, at the time 
of development of the comprehensive plan, parts of the area were zoned LI Light Industrial (either 
in the County or the City), and it appears the Comprehensive Plan intentionally looked to phase 
out industrial development along Southwest Drive (not only south of I-80, but also between I-80 
and Lincolnway). The market has not yet sought to transition existing industrial development to 
residential (or commercial) development in this area, in fact, recent small-scale developments 
have sought to increase storage, warehousing, industrial, or similar uses. No large-scale 
developments have been proposed, and future infrastructure additions (such as water main 
extensions) may change the market pressures in this area significantly. It is likely premature to 
reach a conclusion that residential development in this area has failed. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment largely does not comply with review criterion 1. 
 
2) COMPATIBLY - The proposed amendment is compatible with the surrounding area, and the 
goals and policies of the Plan.  
 
The current situation surrounding this property includes light industrial zoning to the west, north 
and east, and to the southeast. The surrounding development patterns in the industrial-zoned 
areas include industrial-style development to the northwest, northeast, and southeast, with largely 
vacant or civic uses to the west, north, and east. However, I-80 and the BNSF border the property, 
creating barriers to the north and east. 
 
Property to the southwest is zoned Land Use and largely developed with single-family homes. The 
property to the south is zoned MUB and vacant. Property to the north (on the north side of 

PUDC-26-59 
 
 
April 6, 2026 
4 | P a g e 
 
Interstate 80) is shown on the Land Use Plan as “Open Space and Parks” and is presently 
developed as a County Park. The County has been enhancing the park and its facilities over the 
course of the last few years. This Mixed-use Residential area is the residential area closest to the 
designated Park and Open Space area.     
 
The goals of the current future land use map in the surrounding area is to have a mix of 
commercial and residential development everywhere west of the BNSF railroad tracks.  
 
Despite these goals, new developments in the surrounding area have largely been industrial in 
nature, including new industrial development in the LI zone to the northwest and new storage 
uses in new PUD zones to the southwest. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment largely does not comply with review criterion 2. 
   
3) IMPACT MITIGATION - The proposed amendment will address and mitigate impacts on 
transportation, services, and facilities.  
 
Transportation: 
The proposed change will not have any adverse effects on transportation to the subject property. 
This area of the community has significant issues with transportation access. Southwest Drive’s 
connection to Lincolnway is often limited by trains on the railroad tracks. College Drive can also 
be blocked on occasion. WYDOT is in the early stages of developing a proposed bridge 
installation over the BNSF Railway railroad tracks on west College Drive. A public meeting was 
held on March 26, 2026, regarding preliminary design. Tentative construction is anticipated to 
begin in early 2031 with a completion date of 2033. This project is contingent on the award of a 
federal rail crossing elimination grant.  
 
Any development that occurs in this area will have to address transportation issues. Industrial-
style development will have different transportation needs and different funding for infrastructure 
improvements than residential development. 
 
A Major Collector Street is identified on the Transportation Master Plan immediately south of the 
area proposed for change. This street is and Extension of Allison Road between Southwest Drive 
and Parsley Boulevard. Right-of-way dedication and improvements will be required in conjunction 
with future development actions such as platting.  
 
Services: 
The proposed change will not have any adverse effects on provision of services to the subject 
property. This area of the community has significant issues with services, partially due to the 
access issues mentioned above, but also due to lack of water mains. Whatever development 
occurs in this area will have to deal with service issues. Industrial-style development will have 
different service needs and different funding for infrastructure improvements than residential 
development.  
 
Facilities: 
The proposed change will not have any adverse effects on facilities. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criterion 3. 
 

PUDC-26-59 
 
 
April 6, 2026 
5 | P a g e 
 
4) SERVICE PROVISION - The proposed amendment will have minimal effect on service 
provision, including adequacy or availability of urban facilities and services, and is 
compatible with existing and planned service provision  
 
The proposed change will not have any adverse effects on provision of services to the subject 
property. This area of the community has significant issues with services, partially due to the 
access issues mentioned above, but also due to lack of water mains. Whatever development 
occurs in this area will have to deal with service issues. Industrial-style development will have 
different service needs and different funding for infrastructure improvements than residential 
development.  
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) ANNEXATION - The proposed amendment does not jeopardize the City’s ability to annex 
the property. 
 
The subject properties are all currently within City limits.   
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criterion 5. 
     
6) URBAN SERVICES AREA - The proposed amendment is consistent with the logical expansion 
of the Urban Services Area. 
 
This land is currently within the Urban Services Area. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) STRICT ADHERENCE - Strict adherence to the Plan would result in a situation neither 
intended nor in keeping with other key elements and policies of the Plan. 
 
Strict adherence to the Plan would result in residential development adjacent I-80 and a railroad. 
While proximity next to major transportation routes such as this is not always optimal for 
residential development, it does happen with regularity in the Cheyenne region due to 
Cheyenne’s significant lengths of interstates and railroads. Locating industrial zoning near major 
transportation routes would be appropriate, but there would need to be a buffer to existing and 
anticipated residential development to the south and southwest.   
 
The interstates and railroads in this area all existed at the time the comprehensive plan was 
developed, meaning that the original plan likely considered the regional transportation routes in 
the area and came to the conclusion a unified residential neighborhood was appropriate for this 
area. Also, at the time of development of the comprehensive plan, parts of the area were zoned LI 
Light Industrial (either in the County or the City), and it appears the Comprehensive Plan 
intentionally looked to phase out industrial development along Southwest Drive (not only south 
of I-80, but also between I-80 and Lincolnway). 
 
However, the Comprehensive Plan cannot predict the future and encourages responsiveness to 
market demands and monitoring of development trends. Since the passage of the comprehensive 
plan, new land uses in the area have largely been non-residential in nature. Part of the subject 
property has started a landscaping nursery, various new PUDs have allowed storage uses along 

PUDC-26-59 
 
 
April 6, 2026 
6 | P a g e 
 
Southwest Drive in the County, a new City PUD has established a Frontier Days park-n-ride along 
I-25, and various developments north of I-80 have been industrial in nature (in the existing LI 
zone). 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment largely does not comply with review criterion 7. 
 
8) PUBLIC WELFARE AND CONSISTENCY – The proposed plan amendment will promote the 
public welfare and will be consistent with the goals and policies of PlanCheyenne and the 
elements thereof. 
 
No specific use has been identified for this area. Outside of this application, discussion has 
occurred regarding development of a campground or recreational vehicle park, however that 
specific development would not necessarily trigger a land use plan because it can be 
accommodated via conditional use approval in the existing zone districts. Staff recognizes the 
increase in construction activity with numerous large-scale projects and the need for employee 
housing; however, if the end goal of this application is to construct a campground or recreational 
vehicle park, alternative paths to move forward exist and there may be unintended consequences 
of changing the future land use map (and zoning) to accommodate a permissible use in the 
present zone district.  
 
The evaluation of this land use plan amendment and the related zoning should be based on all 
possible alternatives in the desired zone districts supported by the Mixed-use Employment land 
use category.  Once this property is changed to Mixed-use Employment, the entire area might 
domino into industrial development and opportunities for future permanent housing in this area 
may be lost. Housing is a defined need of the community, and it would be detrimental to the 
public welfare to take land anticipated for urban residential development and change it to non-
residential uses.  
 
Policies in PlanCheyenne are mixed on this proposal, but, on the balance, probably would 
encourage maintaining the current land use designation. 
 
Consideration of various policies of PlanCheyenne in regards to this proposal: 
 
• 
Policy 1.1.B. Flexibility to Market Demands: This goal notes that the Future Land Use 
Plan does not predetermine all uses on individual parcels and is meant to provide 
flexibility. The future land use map amendment to Mixed-use Employment will allow for 
more commercial or industrial uses, and may recognize a potential trend of this area 
becoming more industrial in nature.  
• 
 Policy 2.5.A. Community Housing Met: This goal notes that the availability and 
attainability of housing is a concern for the community. Changing the future land use 
map amendment to Mixed-use Employment will reduce the ability for Cheyenne to 
provide available permanent housing.  
• 
Policy 2.5.B. Workforce Housing throughout the Community: This goal encourages 
workforce housing to be dispersed in various locations throughout the community. This 
location is an opportunity to provide that style of housing under the current designation.  
• 
Principle 3.5 Provide opportunities for a range of industrial employment uses: This 
goal encourages Cheyenne to leverage rail and interstate access for a range of industrial 
uses. Large-scale data centers may be utilizing large swaths of existing industrial parks, 
creating the need for new industrial areas located near interstates or railroads. The future

7 | P a g e 
 
land use map amendment to Mixed-use Employment at this location will allow for more 
industrial uses in close proximity to interstates and railroads. 
 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment largely does not comply with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
No other plans are applicable for this area. 
 
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED:__________________________________________ 
 
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the review 
criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application. 
  
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways: 
- 
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on March 21, 2026. 
- 
Mailed Notice. Evidence of mailed notice was provided showing that the letters were mailed on 
March 18, 2026. 
- 
Posted Notice. Evidence of posted notice was provided showing that the signs were posted on 
March 30, 2026. 
- 
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on March 18, 2026. 
 
As of the publication of the Staff Report (March 30, 2026), Staff has received one inquiries or comment 
from the public on this project. The one member of the public called the office and had concerns about 
the traffic impacts of new development. The project page on the website has received 15 views and no 
comments. The project page on the website has received 24 views and no comments. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, review comments were received expressing concerns about the 
appropriateness of the Mixed-use Employment designation at this location. Staff also mentioned drainage 
and traffic concerns that may become necessary to address with the site plan. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Although there are multiple reasons to both approve or deny the request, on the balance, Staff does not 
recommend changing the future land use designation of this area to Mixed-use Employment.  
 
Rationale for approving the request: 
Although the current future land use map encourages rezoning out of the current light industrial zones to 
allow non-industrial uses, most of the recent development in the vicinity has been industrial in nature, 
including storage uses in newly-created County PUD zones along Southwest Drive. LI (and PUD industrial) 
zoning exists within the area proposed to be modified – and on three sides. Also, the LU zone to the 
southwest, although currently developed with residential uses would also allow for light industrial uses 
(via conditional use processes) in the County.   
 
 

PUDC-26-59 
 
 
April 6, 2026 
8 | P a g e 
 
Rationale for denying the request: 
Despite the LI zoning and industrial development existing in the area at the time of the comprehensive 
plan, PlanCheyenne designated all the land surrounded by Lincolnway, the BNSF railroad, College, and  
I-25 with non-industrial future land use designations, expecting the area to transition to residential and 
commercial development. Housing is a need in the Cheyenne region and taking land away from future 
residential development (without adding residential land elsewhere) may further limit opportunities to 
increase housing supply. There is also currently residential development to the south and southwest that 
could be negatively impacted by future industrial development. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan subject to modifications. 
3. Deny the Resolution to not amend the Land Use Plan.  The Planning Commission must conclude 
that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings for denial 
must be stated (Staff Recommendation). 
4. Postpone the Resolution until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite 
specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends denial of the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan for land south of I-80 and east of 
Southwest Dr as shown in Exhibit A from Mixed-use Residential to Mixed-Use Employment.

Background:  
The item for consideration is a Final Plat.  This is approved by Resolution.

• 
Introduction and Referral   
April 13, 2026

April 20, 2026

Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their April 6, 2026 meeting and recommended the 
Governing Body approve this application with the following conditions: 
1. 
Provide a title commitment from a title company or quiet title demonstrating the signatories 
on the plat own all of vacated east-west Frank Court within the Plat boundary. 
2. 
Any vacation of rights-of-way within the plat boundary shall be approved prior to the City 
signatures being affixed to the plat. 
  
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and has not been changed.

from the public on this project. The project page on the website has received 68 views and no 
comments.

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND THE CITY 
CLERK TO SIGN A FINAL PLAT FOR DRY CREEK VISTA, CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING (EAST OF NORTH 
COLLEGE DRIVE AND NORTH OF ROCK SPRINGS STREET)." 
 
 
WHEREAS, JKM Properties LLC has subdivided certain land located east of N College 
Drive and north of Rock Springs Street in accordance with applicable Wyoming Statutes; 
 
 
WHEREAS, JKM Properties LLC, the owners of the above-described land, has caused a 
subdivision plat of said land to be prepared, acknowledged, and certified, particularly describing 
the lots, blocks, easements and rights-of-way contained therein; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the above-described subdivision plat has been presented to the City of 
Cheyenne Planning Commission for consideration, the Planning Commission has recommended 
approval of said plat to the Governing Body, and the plat has been duly executed by the 
Development Office. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the subdivision described as Dry Creek Vista, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, be and the same is hereby approved and confirmed as presented, and that the Mayor 
and the City Clerk are hereby authorized, empowered, and directed to execute the final plat.

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
APRIL 6, 2026 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-26-35: Dry Creek Vista, Final Plat 
REQUEST: 
Subdivision of 3.17 acres proposing creating three residential lots. 
LOCATION: 
East of North College Drive and north of Rock Springs Street. 
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma 
OWNER: 
JKM Properties LLC 
ZONING: 
MR – Medium-density Residential.  
PURPOSE: 
A plat creating three residential Lots.  
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-26-35 
 
 
April 6, 2026 
2 | P a g e 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the Dry Creek Vista Final Plat with staff recommended 
conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
• 
UDC Article 3 Impact Studies 
• 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
• 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a final plat for roughly 3.17 acres of land. The proposed plat would 
create three lots, two of which have existing single-family residences on them. This plat also incorporates 
portions of recently vacated Frank Court. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of final plat, the Commission must make 
the following findings required in UDC 2.1.3.d a detailed analysis is below. The purpose of a final plat 
review is to ensure compliance with the approved preliminary plat and its conditions of approval.   
 
1) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the 
approved preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and 
block layout, and access, and any deviations in the final plat brings the application 
in further compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1)

April 6, 2026 
3 | P a g e 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat that 
was approved on January 26, 2026.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
2) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary 
plat. (UDC 2.1.3.d.2) 
 
The preliminary plat was approved by Planning Commission on January 5, 2026 and 
acknowledged by City Council on January 26, 2026 with the following conditions: 
1. Concurrent with Final Plat Submittal: 
a. 
Provide a vacation map to the City to initiate the full vacation of Frank Court, OR 
include all owners adjacent to Frank Court in the platting action. 
b. Clarify/verify owner and dedication information. 
2. On the Final Plat: 
a. 
Provide necessary easements. 
b. Make minor technical corrections. 
 
The applicant has provided a vacation map for the north-south Frank Court. The vacation is 
currently in front of Council and is scheduled to have final consideration on April 13, 2026.  
 
Staff is still concerned that the ownership of the east-west vacated Frank Court has not been 
appropriately demonstrated. The County confirmed the vacation of east-west Frank Court by 
finally recording a vacation action that was approved by the County in the 1980s. However, this 
action did not specify land ownership. The applicant could provide a title commitment from a title 
company or quiet title demonstrating the signatories on the plat own all of vacated east-west 
Frank Court within the Plat boundary, which would then satisfy this condition.  
 
Easements and technical corrections have been made. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 
3) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. 
(UDC 2.1.3.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. Park fees are calculated at $700 per acre. All fees are due at the time of 
building permit. 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a building 
permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is for an existing development in an area that is already developed. No additional 
streets are proposed. 

PUDC-26-35 
 
 
April 6, 2026 
4 | P a g e 
 
 
 
 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space is required at 750sqft per unit or 8% of the gross development area, whichever is 
less. However, due to the size of the property, plat-level open space is not required. The plat 
includes a common lot which serves as a common open area. 
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any residential building on any 
of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the issuance 
of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED: 
 
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the review 
criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application. 
  
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways: 
- 
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on March 21, 2026. 
- 
Posted Notice Evidence of posted notice was provided showing that the signs were posted on 
February 12, 2026. 
- 
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on February 12, 2026. 
 
As of the publication of the Staff Report (March 30, 2026), Staff has received no inquiries or comments 
from the public on this project. The project page on the website has received 40 views and no comments. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Documentation pertaining to the ownership of east-west Frank Court needs to be 
provided. 
 
 
 

PUDC-26-35 
 
 
April 6, 2026 
5 | P a g e 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of the 
final plat application with conditions. 
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the project as submitted by the applicant. (Staff Recommendation) 
2. Recommend that the application be approved with specific conditions or modifications, and that 
a final plat addressing the conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing 
Body. 
3. Recommend that the application be denied based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add conditions, 
or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the conditions shall 
relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s 
recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on 
the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the Final Plat with the following conditions: 
1. Provide a title commitment from a title company or quiet title demonstrating the signatories on 
the plat own all of vacated east-west Frank Court within the Plat boundary.

3. Master Application Form

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-04-07_TotalExtraction_89363481.pdf
 
 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A 
RESOLUTION 
AMENDING 
CITY 
PROVIDED 
HEALTH 
INSURANCE RELATED BENEFITS FOR SPECIFIED PERMANENT 
PART-TIME CITY EMPLOYEES AND MEMBERS OF THE CITY 
COUNCIL OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE." 
 
WHEREAS, members of the City Council of the City of Cheyenne serve in part-time 
capacities as members of the City Council and are currently eligible for paid term life insurance, 
health and dental insurance, supplemental life insurance, and flexible medical benefits 
reimbursement programs through the City;  
 
WHEREAS, part-time employees of the City of Cheyenne do not qualify for these 
benefits; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body finds that aligning the benefits available to members 
of the City Council with those available to other part-time personnel promotes fairness among 
those who work on behalf of the City in part-time roles. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that members of the City Council, by virtue of their 
status as members of the Council, shall be eligible to enroll in, participate in, or receive City 
provided paid term life insurance, health insurance, dental insurance, supplemental life 
insurance, or flexible medical benefits reimbursement programs offered by or through the City, 
whether such benefits are provided directly or indirectly.  
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that permanent part-time employees who have 
averaged not less than twenty (20) hours of work per week for not less than the previous three 
(3) consecutive years shall be eligible to enroll in, participate in, or receive City provided paid 
term life insurance, health insurance, dental insurance, supplemental life insurance, or flexible 
medical benefits reimbursement programs offered by or through the City, whether such benefits 
are provided directly or indirectly. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that one hundred percent (100%) of the premium owed 
for the respective benefits shall be paid by the respective member of the City Council or part-
time employee. The City shall not pay, reimburse, subsidize, or otherwise bear any portion of 
premiums, costs, expenses, or other financial obligations associated with paid term life 
insurance, health insurance, dental insurance, supplemental life insurance, flexible medical 
benefits reimbursement programs, or any other similar or related benefit for any member of the 
City Council or eligible part-time employee, whether such benefits are provided directly or 
indirectly, or funded in whole or in part by the City; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that any portion of Resolution No. 3652, adopted April 
10, 1995, or other any other resolution, rule, regulation, or policy authorizing members of the 
City Council of the City of Cheyenne or eligible part-time employees to receive these benefits 
9:24 am, Apr 10 2026

 
 
through the City or providing for any City contribution toward such benefits by virtue of their 
membership on the City Council or part-time employee status is hereby repealed to the extent 
they are inconsistent with this Resolution. City staff shall take all necessary administrative, 
contractual, and regulatory actions to amend any applicable rules, regulations, policies, benefit 
plans, and agreements in accordance with this Resolution; and 
 
BE IT FINALLY RESOLVED, that this Resolution shall be effective and applicable, 
with respect to City Council Members, only to City Council Members whose terms of office 
commence in 2027 and thereafter, and shall not affect the compensation or benefits of any 
member during a term of office already in progress as of the effective date of this Resolution. 
Nothing in this Resolution shall be interpreted to affect the benefits available or applicable to 
full-time employees. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF ______________, 
2026. 
 
 
_______________________________________ 
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
____________________________________  
Kylie Soden, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-04-07_TotalExtraction_38780737.pdf
CITY 
TREASURER
 
2101 O’Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
(307) 637-6336 
cheyennecity.org 
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
The Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Cheyenne City Council Members 
FROM: 
 
Robin Lockman, City Treasurer 
DATE: 
 
April 16, 2026 
SUBJECT: 
Reappropriation to be considered for approval 
 
On April 27, 2026, the Governing Body will consider referral of a resolution to the Finance 
Committee to reappropriate funds within the Fiscal Year 2026 City of Cheyenne Budget, pursuant 
to W.S. §§ 16-4-112 and 16-4-113.  This memorandum summarizes the proposed adjustments. 
General Fund Reserves (Total: $118,336) 
▪ 
$18,336 - SeeClickFix ($17,886) and Granicus ($450) software subscription expenses that 
were previously paid with ARPA grant funds, which are no longer available for these items.  
▪ 
$100,000 - Contract #8267 modification to pay for design services for the renovation of the 
former Laramie County Senior Activity Center and the Municipal Court’s second floor, as 
approved by the Governing Body on March 23, 2026. 
Local and State Grant Fund - Additional Revenue (Total:  $2,536,178) 
▪ 
$16,079 – BOPU joint project funding for the 18th Street Storm Sewer Interceptor Project.  
▪ 
$5,500 – Visit Cheyenne grant for out-of-state marketing efforts by the Botanic Gardens, 
Civic Center, and Ice and Events Center.  
▪ 
$2,366,424 – Wyoming State Parks and Cultural Resources funding for the High Plains 
Arboretum renovation and construction project.  
▪ 
$148,175 – Wyoming State Parks and Cultural Resources funding for the High Plains 
Arboretum demolition and abatement project. 
Recreation Programs Fund – Reserves (Total:  $125,000) 
▪ 
$75,000 – conceptual design for Childcare Program renovations and expansion.  
▪ 
$50,000 – additional part-time gymnastics coach salaries. 
Belvoir Ranch Recreation Fund – Reserves (Total:  $100,000) 
▪ 
Costs associated with Contract #7975 (Phase 2 design/build trail), Contract #8098 (trailhead 
and access roads), and necessary site items such as keys, locks, and signage.  
Community Development Block Grant (CDBG) Fund - Additional Revenue (Total:  $78,442) 
▪ 
Reverted FY2024 funds originally allocated to the Lincoln Park Project, now reassigned to 
the Optimist Park Project. 
 

 
2 
 
Real Property Revolving Fund – Reserves (Total:  $150) 
▪ 
Closing costs related to Resolution 6517 for the Holliday Park property sale. 
Law Enforcement Grant Fund - Additional Revenue (Total: $4,000) 
▪ 
U.S. Marshals Service funding for CPD task force overtime. 
Federal Grants Fund - Additional Revenue (Total:  $5,610) 
▪ 
Wyoming Office of Homeland Security pass-through funding for the 18th Street Storm 
Sewer Interceptor Project.  
Transit Fund - Additional Revenue (Total:  $1,414,924) 
▪ 
Federal Transit Administration (FTA) FY2026 funding to support operations, including 
interior and exterior renovations of the new Transit Facility on Westland Road. 
Juvenile Justice Fund - Additional Revenue (Total:  $78,175) 
▪ 
Laramie County contract funding for a new case manager and related operating expenses. 
Annexation Improvements Fund – Reserves (Total: $3,550) 
▪ 
Interest payment for an annexation deposit refund. 
Miscellaneous Agency Fund – Reserves (Total: $1,500) 
▪ 
Education expenses for the Community Risk Reduction (CRR) Program. 
Special Purpose Option Tax Fund – Reserves (Total:  $95,000) 
▪ 
Unanticipated building repairs and improvements at the Botanic Gardens. 
Civic Center Fund - Additional Revenue (Total: $17,542) 
▪ 
Artist payouts. 
Fiduciary Fund - Visit Cheyenne Reserves (Total: $850) 
▪ 
Replacement of a boot facilitated by Arts Cheyenne. 
Perpetual Care Fund – Reserves (Total: $17,000) 
▪ 
Interest transfers to the General Fund for Fiscal Year 2026. 
 
Requested Action 
Please review the above and consider adoption of the attached resolution authorizing these 
appropriations.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2026-04-07_TotalExtraction_86317523.pdf
PLANNING & 
DEVELOPMENT 
2101 O’Neil Avenue 
Cheyenne, WY 82001 
(307) 637-6282 
cheyennecity.org 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White 
Subject: PUDC-26-48 / Cox Ranch – Future Land Use Map Amendment  
Date: 
April 27, 2026 
______________________________________________________________________________________________________________ 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Future Land Use Map Amendment (or Comprehensive Plan 
Amendment).  This is approved by Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
x 
Introduction and Referral   
April 27, 2026 
x 
Public Services Committee  
May 4, 2026 
x 
Final Consideration 
 
May 11, 2026 
 
Planning Commission Recommendation:  
The Planning Commission heard this item at their April 6, 2026, meeting and voted to approve the 
amendment. 
 
The Planning Commission Staff report is attached and unchanged. 
 
Public Comment 
As of the creation of this memo (April 22, 2026), Staff has received no inquires or comments 
from the public on this project. The project page on the website has received 526 views and 15 
comments. Many of the comments are about annexation and not specifically the amendment.  
 
Attachments: 
1. 
Resolution 
2. 
Planning Commission Staff Report 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AMENDING THE FUTURE LAND USE MAP OF 
PLANCHEYENNE CHANGING THE LAND USE DESIGNATION FROM 
URBAN TRANSITION RESIDENTIAL TO MIXED-USE EMPLOYMENT 
FOR PROPERTY GENERALLY LOCATED WEST OF ROUNDTOP 
ROAD AND ADJACENT TO HAPPY JACK ROAD AND AMENDING THE 
URBAN SERVICE BOUNDARY." 
 
 
WHEREAS, PlanCheyenne, as amended by PlanCheyenne: Reflections and Progress 
adopted by the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne by Resolution No. 5573, is the Master 
Plan for the City of Cheyenne and the Comprehensive Plan for portions of Laramie County adopted 
by both the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County Board of County 
Commissioners; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the intent of the Future Land Use plan is not to predetermine specific land 
uses for individual parcels or specific locations for uses or facilities, but to provide a general 
framework for neighborhoods and activity centers organized around roadway networks and major 
parks and open space; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Brad Emmons of AVI, PC, a resident within Cheyenne, has initiated an 
amendment to the Future Land Use map to amend the Urban Service Boundary shown in Exhibit 
A which is attached hereto and incorporated herein and to amend the land use category from Urban 
Transition Residential to Mixed-use Employment for land legally described as: 
 
A Parcel Of Land Located In Township 14 North, Range 67 West Of The 6th P.M., Laramie 
County, Wyoming, Being More Particularly Described As Follows: 
All Of Section 31 Lying North Of The Northerly Right Of Way Of Happy Jack Road (State 
Highway 210) And East Of The Access Easement Granting Ingress And Egress To The Lands 
North In Section 30, And Also Lying Southerly From A Barbed Wire Fence That Runs North Of 
North 1/16th Line Of Section 31 Terminating At The East 1/16th Line, Then Including The 
Northeast 1/4 Of The Northeast 1/4 Of Said Section 31 And Going Southerly To The Northerly 
Right Of Way Of Happy Jack Road (State Highway 210) Plus, The East 1/2 Of The Southeast 1/4 
Of Section 30, Township 14 North, Range 67 West Of The 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming; 
 
Plus, The West 1/2 Of Section 29, Township 14 North, Range 67 West Of The 6th P.M, Laramie 
County, Wyoming; 
 
Plus, All Of Section 32, Except The East 1/2 Of The Northeast 1/4 And The East 1/2 Of The 
Southeast 1/4, Township 14, North Range 67 West Of The 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendments approved by the Governing Body pursuant to the criteria 
set forth on Page 18 of PlanCheyenne, as amended; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Laramie County Board of County Commissioners reviewed the proposed 
future land use map amendment and were given the opportunity to make comments; 
8:36 am, Apr 22 2026

 
 
WHEREAS, a public hearing to receive public input and comments on the proposed Future 
Land Use map amendment for the above-described property was held before the City of Cheyenne 
Planning Commission on April 6, 2026, and the Commission approved the proposed Future Land 
Use map amendment; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Plan Amendment processes and procedures required by PlanCheyenne, as 
amended, have been followed with regard to the request by Brad Emmons to amend the Future 
Land Use map category for the above-described property and amend the Urban Service Boundary 
and Natural and Cultural Resources Area. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne Governing Body hereby amends the 
Land Use Plan for PlanCheyenne changing the land use category from Urban Transition 
Residential to Mixed-use Employment, and amends the Urban Service Boundary, as shown in 
Exhibit A, in accordance with the procedures outlined in PlanCheyenne and Wyoming Statutes § 
15-1-503 and § 15-1-505; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that staff is authorized to amend affected maps of 
PlanCheyenne to reflect the aforementioned changes; 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2026. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
KYLIE SODEN, CITY CLERK

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION

STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-26-48: Future Land Use Plan Amendment adding land to the 
Urban Service Boundary, designating that land Mixed-use Employment 
and, changing land adjacent to that addition from Urban Transition 
Residential to Mixed-use Employment
REQUEST:
A request to amend the Future Land Use Plan, adding land to the Urban 
Service Boundary, designating that land Mixed-use Employment, and 
changing land adjacent to that addition from Urban Transition Residential to 
Mixed-use Employment.
LOCATION:
North of and adjacent to Happy Jack Road, west of Roundtop Road.
APPLICANT:
Brad Emmons, AVI P.C.
OWNER:
Cox, William S Revocable Trust
ZONING:
County LU, proposed rezoning to BP – Business Park following annexation.
PURPOSE:
To match a requested zone change to BP Business Park for development of the 
property.
PREPARED BY:
Connor White, Planner II

PUDC-26-48
April 6, 2026
2 | P a g e
RECOMMENDED MOTION:
Move to approve the Resolution amending the Future Land Use Plan for 1,193.4 acres of land west of 
Roundtop Road and north of Happy Jack Road as shown in the Future Land Use Map provided, adding 
land to the Urban Service Boundary, designating that land Mixed-use Employment, and changing land 
adjacent to that addition from Urban Transition Residential to Mixed-use Employment. 
   
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS:
x
PlanCheyenne (2014 Community Plan)
BACKGROUND:
The applicant is requesting amending the Future Land Use Map for roughly 1,193.4 acres of land to Mixed-use 
Employment to accommodate a zone change to the BP – Business Park zone.
Part of this property is outside the Urban Services Boundary and has no Future Land Use Map designation. This 
area is proposed to be added to the Urban Services Boundary and designated Mixed-use Employment.
The portion of the property currently within the Urban Service Boundary is shown as Urban Transition
Residential in the Future Land Use Plan. The Urban Transition Residential category, along with the Rural 
Residential category, provides a gradual transition from the urbanized areas of the Cheyenne Area to the
rural areas on the periphery. It includes existing homes and neighborhoods, as well as some planned and 
recent new growth areas.
A concurrent application for a zoning map amendment has been submitted. The zoning map amendment will 
accompany the future land use amendment at City Council. If the application for the zoning map amendment is 
approved, the applicant will be required to submit a site plan application for any new development.

ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA:
Should the Planning Commission wish to approve an amendment to the Future Land Use Map of the 
Comprehensive Plan, the Commission must make the following considerations outlined in the 2014
Community Plan (page 18).  A detailed analysis is below:
1) BENEFICIAL OR ADDRESSES ISSUES - The proposed amendment is beneficial to the 
community or addresses issues not foreseen during the planning process.
The proposed amendment to Mixed-use Employment at the proposed location would be 
beneficial as it is on the very edge of the community. Mixed-use Employment areas should be 
located toward the fringe of the community and in areas that are not highly residential as they 
allow for more industrial and business park type developments. This area is mostly agricultural in 
use at this time and is at the edge of the urban service boundary. The nearest residential 
subdivision is a rural-residential style development a half-mile to the east.
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 1.
2) COMPATIBLY - The proposed amendment is compatible with the surrounding area, 
and the goals and policies of the Plan. 
The subject property is adjacent to Mixed-use Employment to south, no designation to the west, 
Agricultural/Rural to the north and Urban Transition Residential to the east. The Urban Transition
Residential would not allow for commercial or industrial type uses. Higher intensity employment 
is encouraged in the core of Mixed-Use Employment Campus areas, or adjacent to collector or 
arterial roadways. The proposed amendment is consistent with the goals and policies of 
PlanCheyenne, as discussed in more detail below.
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 2.
  
3) IMPACT MITIGATION - The proposed amendment will address and mitigate 
impacts on transportation, services, and facilities.
Transportation:
The transportation network in this area of the City is somewhat developed with a major street to 
the south. All impacts to the transportation network will be addressed through the platting and 
site plan processes. Additional rights-of-way could be required depending on the development 
pattern proposed for the property.
Services:
The proposed change will not have any adverse effects on the provision of services to the subject 
property. Service providers, such as the fire department, will be included in platting and site 
planning processes to assess service needs.
Facilities:
The proposed change will likely require sewer to be extended to the development area.
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 3.

PUDC-26-48
April 6, 2026
4 | P a g e
4) SERVICE PROVISION - The proposed amendment will have minimal effect on 
service provision, including adequacy or availability of urban facilities and services, 
and is compatible with existing and planned service provision 
This land is currently proposed to be annexed into City limits and urban facilities and services are 
available to the subject property. Sewer would need to be extended from the south.  
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 4.
5) ANNEXATION - The proposed amendment does not jeopardize the City’s ability to 
annex the property.
The subject property is currently proposed to be annexed into City limits.  
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 5.
.  
6) URBAN SERVICES AREA - The proposed amendment is consistent with the logical 
expansion of the Urban Services Area.
This land is about 70% within the Urban Services Area. The Urban Service Boundary in this area is 
a straight north-south line that does not follow topography or current parcel boundaries. This 
future land use amendment would extend the Urban Service Area to match the platted boundary 
of the land. 
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 6.
7) STRICT ADHERENCE - Strict adherence to the Plan would result in a situation 
neither intended nor in keeping with other key elements and policies of the Plan.
Strict adherence to the Plan may result in unintended consequences. While the land could 
develop with residential, it is on the edge of the community where the higher level of services 
typically expected with urban residential development could be challenging to expand. 
Alternatively, the area could develop with large-acre ranchette-style development in the County; 
however, such development is not optimal for areas within the Urban Service Boundary.
The current Urban Service Boundary in this area appears to have been created by reaching the 
edge of the 2014 ‘Planning Boundary’. Everywhere else, the Urban Service Boundary follows 
topography or parcel boundaries. Strict adherence to the current Urban Service Boundary in this 
area of the community may be unfairly limiting to land otherwise developable.
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 7.
8) PUBLIC WELFARE AND CONSISTENCY – The proposed plan amendment will 
promote the public welfare and will be consistent with the goals and policies of 
PlanCheyenne and the elements thereof.
Staff is satisfied that the proposed application will not have any adverse effects on public welfare. 
Furthermore, the application accomplishes the following policies contained within PlanCheyenne:

PUDC-26-48
April 6, 2026
5 | P a g e
x
Policy 1.1.B: Flexibility to Respond to Market Demands
The Future Land Use Plan does not predetermine all land uses on individual parcels and is 
intended to provide some flexibility for the market to help determine which uses are 
needed at a given time. However, when evaluating land uses for a proposed
development, consider whether a diverse and integrated mix of uses is provided
throughout the entire Cheyenne urbanized area.
x
Policy 3.1.B: Recruit New Employers
Work with partners, such as the Chamber and LEADS, to continue to recruit larger 
employers that provide family wage jobs and that are predominantly clean, nonpolluting
industries.
x
Policy 3.5.A: Locations for Heavy Industrial Uses
Continue to leverage the Cheyenne Area’s unique location and physical assets, such as 
heavy rail and interstate access, by designating and maintaining areas for heavy industrial 
uses that may not be suitable within designated activity centers due to design and/or 
traffic impacts. Such uses may include trucking and distribution facilities, oil and gas 
support services, and warehousing among others.
The proposed Future Land Use amendment complies with review criteria 8.
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS:
No other plans are applicable for this area.  
PUBLIC NOTICE AND PUBLIC COMMENTS RECEIVED:__________________________________________
Although projects have public notice and the opportunity for public comments, projects are required to 
be approved or denied based on the review criteria (listed above). Public comments relevant to the review 
criteria may assist the review and discussion on the application.
The public was given notice of the project in the following ways:
-
Legal Notice in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle published on March 21, 2026.
-
Posted Notice Evidence of posted notice was provided showing that the signs were posted on 
March 30, 2026.
-
Internet Posting on connect.cheyennecity.org on February 16, 2026.
As of the publication of the Staff Report (March 30, 2026), Staff has received no inquiries. There have been 
14 comments made on Connect Cheyenne. Many of the comments are from people who have the 
misimpression that they are being annexed by the City. Other comments are generic and state they do 
not agree with this project. The project page on the website has received 292 views.
CONCLUSION:
Staff recommends approval of the extension of the Urban Service Boundary and the Mixed-use 
Employment Land Use designation for this property because it is located on the edge of the 
community and is more than a half mile from any residential neighborhoods. A future land use 

PUDC-26-48
April 6, 2026
6 | P a g e
amendment to Mixed-use Employment will support the applicants proposed zoning map 
amendment and allow for further development.
ALTERNATIVES:
1.
Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan as submitted by the applicant and expanded 
upon by staff (Staff Recommendation).
2.
Approve the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan subject to modifications.
3.
Deny the Resolution to not amend the Land Use Plan.  The Planning Commission must conclude 
that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings for denial 
must be stated.
4.
Postpone the Resolution until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved.
General Information Regarding Alternatives:
In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation, the Commission shall cite specific 
reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial.
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:
Staff recommends approval of the Resolution amending the Land Use Plan for 1,193.4 acres of land west 
of Roundtop Road and north of Happy Jack Road as shown in the applicant’s submitted Future Land Use 
map, adding land to the Urban Service Boundary, designating that land Mixed-use Employment, and 
changing land adjacent to that addition from Urban Transition Residential to Mixed-use Employment.

RE:  
FUTURE LAND USE AMENDMENT OF LAND ON HAPPY JACK ROAD 
Dear Mr. Bloom 
 
Please accept this request to amend the Future Land Use Amendment for property currently 
under consideration for Annexation within the City of Cheyenne. 
 
The property is currently a vacant parcel just west and south of 8802 Happy Jack Road.  It is 
requested to be changed from rural residential to mixed use employment.  With annexation of 
the property and the extension of water and sewer to the property rural residential would not be 
a logical designation.  The request for mixed use employment is consistent with the properties 
to the south and east and the continued development of business parks to the west of the 
current City of Cheyenne boundaries. 
 
The requested amendment is consistent with the broader vision of the City’s Comprehensive 
Plan by encouraging orderly development and thoughtful land use planning.  The area has seen 
growth in commercial development of the North Range Business Park which make the proposed 
designation appropriate. 
 
If you have any questions or would like to discuss this project in greater detail, please contact 
me directly. 
  
 
Respectfully Submitted

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION APPROPRIATING EXCESS FUNDS FROM THE 
NOVEMBER 2, 2021, ONE PERCENT (1%) SPECIFIC PURPOSE SALES 
AND USE TAX FOR THE ACQUISITION OF REAL PROPERTY.” 
 
WHEREAS, at the special election held in Laramie County on November 2, 2021, voters 
approved specific purposes to be funded by a one percent (1%) specific purpose sales and use tax; 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 39-15-211, the Governing Body may appropriate its 
proportionate share of excess funds from such tax for other specific purposes authorized by a 
majority vote of the Governing Body, provided such appropriation does not fund the ordinary 
operations of local government; 
 
WHEREAS, final accounting and reconciliation of revenues from the November 2, 2021 
tax are not yet complete, with completion anticipated to occur in May or June 2026; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has identified the property commonly known as the 
former Senior Center, located at 2101 Thomes Avenue and owned by the Cheyenne Housing 
Authority, as suitable for municipal purposes; 
 
WHEREAS, the property’s proximity to the City’s municipal building will provide 
operational efficiencies, improved public access to services, and enhanced coordination of City 
functions; 
 
WHEREAS, acquisition and renovation of the existing structure is more cost-effective than 
acquisition and construction of a new facility, and therefore represents a fiscally responsible use 
of public funds; 
 
WHEREAS, based on historical performance of comparable ballot measures, including 
approximately Five Million Seven Hundred Twenty-Nine Thousand Four Hundred Fifty-Two 
Dollars ($5,729,452.00) in excess funds generated from the 2017 specific purpose sales and use 
tax, the Governing Body finds that sufficient excess revenues are expected to be available to fund 
acquisition of the Senior Center; and 
 
WHEREAS, the use of excess specific purpose sales and use tax revenues for the 
acquisition of real property constitutes a capital expenditure and does not fund ordinary 
governmental operations, in compliance with Wyo. Stat. § 39-15-203(a)(iii)(B). 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that, to the extent excess funds are available following final 
reconciliation, excess tax funds from the November 2, 2021 one percent (1%) specific purpose 
sales and use tax in an amount not to exceed One Million One Hundred Seventy-Seven Thousand 
Sixty-Two Dollars and Ninety-Seven Cents ($1,177,062.97) are hereby appropriated for the 
purpose of acquiring the real property located at 2101 Thomes Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming, from 
the Cheyenne Housing Authority. 
8:29 am, Apr 21, 2026

 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this Resolution shall be effective upon passage and 
execution by the Mayor and City Clerk.

2026.

WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming (the “City”), is the designated recipient of 
Federal Transit Administration (“FTA”) funds for the Cheyenne Transit Program; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Transit Program (“CTP”) provides public transit services for 
the Cheyenne urban area with both fixed route and curb to curb service; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization (“MPO”), the City of 
Cheyenne, and the Wyoming Department of Transportation (“WYDOT”) have entered into a 
Consolidated Planning Grant Agreement to provide operational planning for the CTP; 
 
WHEREAS, CTP receives Federal Transit Administration (“FTA”) Urbanized Area 
Formula Program 5307 Operating and Capital funding and therefore is subject to the requirements 
of Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 and the United States Department of Transportation’s 
implementing regulations; 
 
WHEREAS, the MPO has been assigned to perform the duties of Title VI Officer for the 
CTP; 
 
WHEREAS, the 2025 Updated Title VI Program ensures that no person shall be excluded 
from participation in, denied the benefits of, or otherwise subjected to discrimination in the receipt 
of CTP services on the basis of race, color, national origin, age, or any other characteristic 
protected by applicable law; 
 
WHEREAS, the FTA requires that all direct and primary recipients document their 
compliance by submitting a Title VI Program to their FTA Regional Civil Rights Officer once 
every three (3) years or as otherwise directed; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City must formally adopt the Title VI Program 
prior to its submission to the FTA. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Title VI Program for the Cheyenne Transit Program, a 
copy of which is on file with CTP, is hereby approved and adopted.
